Consumer inflation rises faster than expected in October, CPI report shows

10 Nov, 19:06 IST

The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported that the consumer price index increased 0.4% on a monthly basis in October. LSEG polled economists who expected the consumer price index to rise 0.3% from the prior month. Core prices were up 0.3% in October, which was slightly higher than the 0.2% estimate of economists polled by LSEG.

Read the full report

The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported that the consumer price index increased 0.4% on a monthly basis in October. LSEG polled economists who expected the consumer price index to rise 0.3% from the prior month. Core prices were up 0.3% in October, which was slightly higher than the 0.2% estimate of economists polled by LSEG.

Energy prices rose 0.7% on a monthly basis in October, while gasoline prices increased 1%. Food prices were up 0.5% from the prior month in October, marking the largest increase for the broader index since April. EY-Parthenon chief economist Gregory Daco stated that inflation surprised to the upside in October with broad-based pressures.

Navy Federal Credit Union chief economist Heather Long noted that the implied rate is around 4%, which is well above wage gains of 3.1% in the past year. Fox Business ↗

The U.S. Consumer Price Index increased 0.4 per cent in October after advancing 0.6 per cent in September. Food prices surged 0.5 per cent in October after barely rising in the prior three months. Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said on Tuesday that the U.S. central bank should raise interest rates next month.

11 Nov, 03:44 IST

The U.S. Department of Energy increased its 2026 average price forecast for Brent crude oil by 1.2% to $97.44 per barrel from the previous $96.32 per barrel. The U.S. Energy Information Administration, operating under the Department of Energy, released the report containing the oil price forecast. China crude oil imports hit seven-month high in October

Baird Maritime · 10 Nov, 15:56 IST

China's customs data released on Tuesday showed that October crude oil imports reached 41.94 million tonnes, equating to 9.88 million barrels per day. China's daily crude import rate in October increased by 9.4 per cent compared to September. China's crude oil imports in October decreased by 13.3 per cent compared to the same period a year earlier.

Energy Aspects estimated that China's refinery throughput dropped to 13.75 million barrels per day in October. Energy Aspects estimated that China's crude inventories increased by six million barrels in October. China's customs data reported that oil product exports dropped to 5.18 million tonnes in October from 7.16 million tonnes in September.

China's natural gas imports grew by 2.4 per cent year-on-year to reach 10.01 million tonnes in October. Baird Maritime ↗

Excelerate targeting cruise ship LNG demand in Bahamas, considering bunkering

www.rivieramm.com, Hydrocarbon Engineering · 10 Nov, 22:05 IST

Excelerate Energy secured an 11-year agreement to supply AxDo Bahamas with liquefied natural gas for a planned hub near Grand Bahama. Excelerate Energy will supply up to 16 trillion British thermal units of liquefied natural gas annually to AxDo. AxDo is a joint venture between Axpo, a Swiss energy group, and Domicisle LNG, a Bahamas-based energy and maritime logistics company.

Excelerate Energy CEO Steven Kobos stated that the 16 trillion British thermal units annual supply agreement represents less than half the planned asset capacity. Initial deliveries to the hub are expected to begin in 2027 utilizing an existing liquefied natural gas supply and logistics platform in Jamaica. A second phase for the hub, planned for the second half of 2028, involves deploying a floating storage unit near Grand Bahama subject to conditions.

Steven Kobos identified the Bahamas as the largest potential source of liquefied natural gas consumption among Caribbean locations assessed by Excelerate Energy. Excelerate Energy anticipates the Bahamas project will generate approximately US$6M in EBITDA in 2027. www.rivieramm.com ↗

Strait of Hormuz LNG traffic hits highest in October since Iran war began

The Hindu Business · 10 Nov, 10:18 IST

In October 2026, the monthly tally of laden liquefied natural gas tankers exiting the Strait of Hormuz reached its highest level since the U.S.-Iran conflict began. According to S&P Global Energy data, 31 liquefied natural gas tankers passed through the waterway in October, consisting of 23 from Qatar's Ras Laffan and eight from the United Arab Emirates' Das Island. October exports increased compared to the 23 vessels that exited the strait in September.

S&P Global Energy senior principal analyst Eric Yep stated that the United Arab Emirates has returned to pre-war transit levels while Qatar is at over 30% of pre-war transits. Kpler shiptracking data indicated that 42 liquefied natural gas cargoes were loaded in October, rising from 27 cargoes in September. Ras Laffan October loadings reached 35 cargoes totaling over 2.7 million tons, marking the highest volume since the war began according to Kpler data.

Two vessels carrying liquefied natural gas from Ras Laffan performed ship-to-ship transfers off Oman's Sohar anchorage, involving the SC Prestige, the BW ENN Crystal Sky, the Al Mafyar, Shandong Redwood, and Ejnan. The Hindu Business ↗

Gold’s Purchasing Power Against U.S. Real Estate Over Time

Visual Capitalist · 10 Nov, 21:07 IST

Since 2000, the price of gold has increased more than 16-fold, outpacing U.S. home price growth according to data from Macrotrends and the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. In 2000, the median price of a new U.S. home was $167,550, which was equivalent to about 606 ounces of gold priced at $277 per ounce. By the first half of 2026, gold reached $4,514 per ounce and the median new U.S. home price reached approximately $409,600.

By 2026, the equivalent of 606 ounces of gold had the buying power of nearly seven homes. MSA Drilling Confirms Linked High-grade Polymetallic Breccias - Porphyry Cu-Mo-Au-Ag Centre: Drills 186 m of 0.48% Copper, 0.18 g/t Gold and 10.1 g/t Silver to End of Hole – Company Announcement - FT.com

Financial Times · 11 Nov, 04:00 IST

Minsud Resources reported assay results from hole CHDH26-174, which is the first hole of a 10,500 m diamond drilling program at the Chita Valley project located in San Juan Province, Argentina. Drill hole CHDH26-174 intercepted 696 m of continuous sulphide mineralisation from a depth of 50 m to the end of the hole at 746 m. Dr. Renato Bobis, Vice President of Exploration at Minsud Resources, stated that the drill hole confirms continuing high-grade breccias and porphyry mineralisation with geochemistry indicating the system gets hotter with depth.

Drill hole CHDH26-174 passed through three stacked mineralised systems, transitioning downwards from a high-sulphidation zone, through an intermediate-sulphidation zone, into a deep high-grade breccia and molybdenum-rich porphyry environment. India's natural interest rate is expected to stay stable despite rising rates in developed economies, according to a study by SBI Capital Markets. The narrowing gap between Indian and US bond yields could hinder India's reliance on foreign investments to fund its current account deficit. In the United States, the natural rate of interest is projected to increase due to rising government debt, higher borrowing costs, and shifts in global investment flows.

Inflation-driven interest rate increases accounted for about 75-85 per cent of the rise in US Treasury yields since mid-2020. The gap between inflation in India and the US has narrowed over the past decade, shrinking the difference in policy rates and government bond yields.

USA and Americas

Federal Reserve Research Reveals New Inflation Threat

Yahoo Finance · 10 Nov, 22:54 IST

Federal Reserve economists revealed that oil prices now have a much greater impact on underlying inflation than they did before the pandemic. Fed economists Andrea De Michelis, Musa Orak, Avani Pradhan and Albert Queralto published the findings in a November 9 research note examining five advanced economies. Before the pandemic, a 10-percentage-point rise in relative oil-price inflation increased core inflation by an estimated 0.01 percentage point.

After the pandemic, the impact of a 10-percentage-point relative oil-price inflation increase on core inflation grew to 0.14 percentage point. Researchers' model projects that average core inflation across advanced economies could reach approximately 2.8% in the year 2027. Researchers emphasized that their model's projection of 2.8% core inflation in 2027 is an upper-bound scenario instead of an official Federal Reserve forecast.

In a simulated 10% oil-price shock, the resulting gross domestic product damage was approximately twice as severe as under pre-pandemic conditions. Yahoo Finance ↗

France has accused Iran of a cyberattack that targeted the refrigeration systems of approximately 700 supermarkets nationwide. During the attack on October 12, refrigerator temperatures increased to 50 degrees Celsius for around six hours. The Intermarché supermarket group reported experiencing millions of euros in losses in a single day due to discarded food.

France's national cybersecurity agency ANSSI blamed the 'Shahid Kaveh' group, which is affiliated with Iran's paramilitary Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps. French Foreign Minister Jean-Noël Barrot summoned the Iranian ambassador to lodge a strong protest and announced plans to impose European sanctions. Paris prosecutor Laura Becouau stated that preparations are underway to issue international arrest warrants for the leaders and associates involved.

Supermarkets in Sweden, Belgium, Luxembourg, Ireland, and Estonia also encountered refrigerator and freezer problems suspected to be cyberattacks. The Paris prosecutor’s office initiated a judicial investigation and is pursuing international arrest warrants for the alleged unit leader and two deputies. French prosecutors identified the group as Shahid Kaveh, linking it to the Guards’ electronic and cyber center through Farsi-language activity and Iran-traced IP addresses. ‘Flying blind’: Why experts fear Treasury’s China biotech investment crackdown will backfire

Fierce Biotech · 10 Nov, 20:00 IST

The U.S. Department of the Treasury proposed rules to implement the Comprehensive Outbound Investment National Security Act, aiming to add pharmaceutical compounds to an outbound investment blacklist targeting China. Peter Kolchinsky, managing partner at RA Capital, stated that the proposed restrictions would cripple American innovation, hand dominance to Europe and China, and leave investors flying blind. RA Capital and a coalition of industry leaders published a formal comment letter warning that the proposed rule constrains only American companies while allowing foreign competitors to scoop up Chinese clinical assets.

The Treasury draft policy avoids banning straightforward, cash-based drug licensing deals but casts an ambiguous net over equity investments, co-development arrangements, and joint ventures with Chinese entities. Deborah Curtis, a partner at Arnold & Porter, explained that COINS restrictions apply to any U.S. person, making it easier for foreign firms to evade them by cutting Americans out of the loop. India Sugar Prices: Full Details

JournalArta, ABC News & Headlines – Australian Broadcasting Corporation · 11 Nov, 02:47 IST

India's Department of Consumer Affairs stated that sugar prices rose by over 30 per cent in August compared to July due to weak monsoon rains straining sugarcane crops. Sugarcane juice seller Dasappa reported that sugarcane now costs him 80 rupees per kilogram compared to 40 rupees previously, leading him to charge 25 rupees for a cup up from about 10 rupees last year. The United Nations Food and Agriculture Organization reported that global sugar prices increased by 6.6 per cent in October, marking the fourth consecutive monthly rise and the highest level since April of last year.

India's federal government lifted sugar tariffs to permit duty-free imports of up to 1 million tonnes until the end of the year to increase supply and lower prices before the Hindu festival season. The Karnataka state government is providing about $50 million in support to farmers along with an extra $1.70 for each tonne of sugarcane delivered to mills. Kurubar Shanta Kumar, president of the Karnataka State Sugarcane Growers Association, stated that the crop yield dropped by 9 million tonnes compared to the previous year.

Bajaj Hindusthan Sugar, one of India's largest sugar factories, reported a production decline of almost 11 per cent in the last financial year. JournalArta ↗

‘Flying blind’: Why experts fear Treasury’s China biotech investment crackdown will backfire

Fierce Biotech · 10 Nov, 20:00 IST

The U.S. Department of the Treasury proposed rules to implement the Comprehensive Outbound Investment National Security Act, aiming to add pharmaceutical compounds to an outbound investment blacklist targeting China. Peter Kolchinsky, managing partner at RA Capital, stated that the proposed restrictions would cripple American innovation, hand dominance to Europe and China, and leave investors flying blind. RA Capital and a coalition of industry leaders published a formal comment letter warning that the proposed rule constrains only American companies while allowing foreign competitors to scoop up Chinese clinical assets.

The Treasury draft policy avoids banning straightforward, cash-based drug licensing deals but casts an ambiguous net over equity investments, co-development arrangements, and joint ventures with Chinese entities. Deborah Curtis, a partner at Arnold & Porter, explained that COINS restrictions apply to any U.S. person, making it easier for foreign firms to evade them by cutting Americans out of the loop. Europe

Philip R. Lane: The science and practice of monetary policy communication: the ECB experience

ECB press · 10 Nov, 22:15 IST

Philip R. Lane stated that monetary policy communication steers financial market participants' expectations and shapes financing conditions across maturity and risk spectrums. Large language models and machine learning techniques allow automated analysis of large volumes of text by evaluating the context in which words are used. European Central Bank research shows that internal measures of communication tone track shifts in the deposit facility rate, turning hawkish during tightening and dovish during easing.

Research from the European Central Bank indicates that information extracted from monetary policy statements improves forecasts of euro area core inflation several quarters ahead. Data presented from the European Central Bank's 2021 monetary policy strategy review indicates that information regarding the new inflation target reached approximately one in ten consumers following its announcement. European Central Bank research demonstrates that unexpected shifts in monetary policy communication generally have smaller and slower macroeconomic effects than unexpected interest rate decisions.

European Central Bank meeting day monetary policy surprises can be partially inferred from euro area equity market movements over the preceding six weeks. ECB press ↗
